--- Commit Message ---
LU-19921 target: verify GSSIAM tokens on the target

When the target receives a SEC_CTX_INIT RPC, it calls
tgt_gssiam_verify() to authenticate the token with the external
GSSIAM server via the l_gssiam_auth upcall. Once authenticated,
the token and its resolved identity/permissions are cached and
attached to the export.

This patch includes:
- Target GSSIAM verification framework (tgt_gssiam.c) and upcall
  cache interactions to validate tokens and manage the gssiam_server
  cache.
- l_gssiam_auth utility and man page to perform external
  authentication and downcall results to the kernel.
- Remote authentication forwarding via MDS_GET_INFO RPCs
  (tgt_gssiam_rpc_authenticate, mdt_get_info_gssiam_authenticate,
  ofd_gssiam_authenticate_remote) delegating verification to MDT0.
- Dynamic per-identity nodemap creation and property configuration
  (gssiam_get_nodemap) derived from the authenticated principal.
- Wire format definition (struct gssiam_auth_rep) in lustre_idl.h
  along with corresponding wiretest entries.
- Move DEFAULT_NODEMAP definition from nodemap_internal.h to
  lustre_nodemap.h as LUSTRE_DEFAULT_NODEMAP for cross-subsystem
  access.
- Rework tgt_mod_init() error-unwind paths in tgt_main.c to ensure
  complete rollback on initialization failures, including
  tgt_gssiam_init().

Signed-off-by: Di Wang <ddiwang@google.com>
Change-Id: I9fbd92e7e5dcdbc0039f90f547ce3841b005615e

--- Message (Gerrit AI review for Lustre, 2026-08-06 02:53:16.000000000) ---
Patch Set 64:

(8 comments)

AI code review results below, please doublecheck

Some concerns, see inline comments. The main one is that nothing in this series registers the sysfs tunables for the new "gssiam_server" upcall cache, so the local (MDT0) authentication path has no downcall to complete it and every GSSIAM mount stalls for the 60s acquire timeout and fails; that needs either the tunables patch ordered before this one or the interface added here. There is also no test exercising the new authentication path (a sanity-sec/conf-sanity case that mounts with the gssiam flavor and checks the resulting nodemap/projid would cover most of it).

--- Message (Gerrit AI review for Lustre, 2026-08-11 16:04:27.000000000) ---
Patch Set 72:

(11 comments)

AI code review results below, please doublecheck

Some concerns, see inline comments. The main one is that nothing registers sysfs entries for the new "gssiam_server" upcall cache, so l_gssiam_auth cannot find its downcall file and no local authentication can ever complete - as far as I can tell that blocks every GSSIAM path, including the ones that go through MDT0 by RPC. There is also no test coverage: this patch adds a mock authentication binary whose stated purpose is to stand in for the real IAM service, which looks like exactly what a sanity-sec test_NNN could drive end to end.

--- Message (Gerrit AI review for Lustre, 2026-08-25 16:47:54.000000000) ---
Patch Set 91:

(13 comments)

AI code review results below, please doublecheck

Some significant concerns, see inline comments. The main ones are that the shipped l_gssiam_auth helper approves every token unconditionally, that nothing in the tree registers the sptlrpc/gssiam_server/gssiam_auth_downcall parameter the helper writes to, and that the new MDS_GET_INFO authentication handler is reachable from any connected client.

There is also no test coverage: nothing under lustre/tests/ references gssiam anywhere in this series, so none of the target-side verification, the MDT0 upcall, or the MDT/OST forwarding RPC is exercised. A sanity-sec case covering a GSSIAM mount against MDT0 and against a remote OST would be worth adding here.
